# Claim ledger: contests levels 1 to 3, audit round 2

Blind read of `app/content/courses/contests/` at e69cac72: l1 to l12, review-1 to review-3, homework-1 to homework-3. One row per prose block, statement console, graded key, slip, option and its `why`, hint, feedback line, `explain`, code widget (solution, tests, `how`), long-form model and rubric line, and summary line.

Rows: 2686. Verdicts: correct 2680, wrong 2, imprecise 4, misleading 0, unverified 0.

Basis shorthand. "script part A" and "sweep" are `docs/reports/contests-L1-3-concepts-r2.py` (part A: recomputation and simulation; part B: the `test-N` sweep, run with `--pkgs` under the page's Pyodide 0.27.7 through `app/tools/code-runner.mjs`). A sweep entry reads `"coding" -> first failing test`. "Timing-dependent" marks a result that rests on the 5 s limit and Pyodide's speed. The named sources are Knuth, *The Art of Computer Programming* (TAOCP); Hardy and Wright, *An Introduction to the Theory of Numbers*; Kleinberg and Tardos, *Algorithm Design*; Cormen et al., *Introduction to Algorithms* (CLRS); Graham, Knuth and Patashnik, *Concrete Mathematics*; Engel, *Problem-Solving Strategies*; Laaksonen, *Competitive Programmer's Handbook* (CPH); Halim, *Competitive Programming 3*; the Python documentation and cppreference.

| l2-limits-to-complexity | summary.trap | One loop is not one step per item: `x in some_list` scans the list, so the loop is $O(n^2)$. | correct | As screens 1 to 8 |

| l5-modulo | summary.ideas[0] | Addition, subtraction and multiplication respect remainders: reduce after every step, and nothing grows. | correct | As screens 2 to 8 |
| l5-modulo | summary.ideas[1] | Division does not. Modulo a prime $p$, multiply by the inverse $b^{p-2} \bmod p$; 0 has no inverse. | correct | As screens 2 to 8 |
| l5-modulo | summary.ideas[2] | Repeated squaring computes $a^e \bmod m$ in $O(\log e)$ multiplications; in C++, keep residues in `long long` and add $m$ before `%` on a difference. | correct | As screens 2 to 8 |
| l5-modulo | summary.formula | Inverse modulo a prime: a^{-1} \equiv a^{p-2} \pmod p \quad (p \nmid a) (From Fermat's little theorem, $a^{p-1} \equiv 1$.) | correct | As screens 2 to 8 |
| l5-modulo | summary.trap | `(r // b) % p` divides a remainder and gives nonsense. Multiply by `pow(b, p - 2, p)` instead. | correct | As screens 2 to 8 |

| l11-bitmasks | summary.ideas[0] | A mask is an integer whose bit $i$ says whether item $i$ is in, so `range(1 << n)` visits every subset. | correct | As screens 1, 2, 9 |
| l11-bitmasks | summary.ideas[1] | `mask >> i & 1`, `mask \| 1 << i`, `mask & -mask` and a popcount read, add, isolate and count items. | correct | As screens 1, 2, 9 |
| l11-bitmasks | summary.ideas[2] | Small $n$ in the constraints signals $2^n$; a DP over masks or meet in the middle stretches it further. | correct | As screens 1, 2, 9 |
| l11-bitmasks | summary.formula | Subsets and submasks: \sum_{\text{mask}} 2^{\text{popcount(mask)}} = 3^n (Every subset is $O(2^n)$; every submask of every mask is $O(3^n)$.) | correct | As screens 1, 2, 9 |
| l11-bitmasks | summary.trap | `1 << n - 1` is `1 << (n - 1)` in Python: it skips every subset holding the last item. | correct | As screens 1, 2, 9 |

| review-2 | items[2].explain.text | $p + 1 = 1{,}000{,}000{,}008$ is divisible by 3, so $(p + 1)/3$ is the inverse. Fermat's `pow(3, p - 2, p)` gives the same. | correct | script part A |
